CEO Response to Evaluations


CEO Tafadzwa Ndlovu responded to the evaluations and questions:

"Tafadzwa Ndlovu Thank you all for your candid, detailed feedback and for your commitment to a thorough and substantive annual review. I appreciate the directness with which you’ve highlighted both strengths and areas for improvement—this is exactly the level of engagement and scrutiny we need to drive the company forward.

1. Feedback Themes

Across your evaluations, several common themes have emerged:

**A. Decisive, Clear Leadership and Communication**
You consistently recognized my clarity in articulating strategy and rationale, my ability to keep meetings focused, and my decisiveness in driving action even amid split votes. I value this feedback and will continue to lead with directness and practical clarity.

**B. Need for Greater Shareholder Engagement and Consensus-Building**
A clear concern is my tendency to override split votes, which, while ensuring momentum, has sometimes come at the expense of broader consensus and buy-in. Several of you noted that this approach risks alienating more cautious shareholders and undermining long-term collaboration.

**C. Operational Discipline and Risk Management**
You have been unified in calling out the need for more explicit, actionable plans to address operational discipline and execution risk—especially after underperforming quarters. While our growth focus produced tangible results, the flat Q2 and missed opportunities in other asset classes exposed gaps that must be addressed.

---

2. Responses to Specific Shareholder Questions

**From Darael Mukendi:**
*1. Steps to address operational discipline and build trust without sacrificing momentum:*
Next year, I will implement a quarterly operational review process—after each investment, we will conduct a structured debrief to identify execution gaps and assign clear, accountable actions for improvement. I will also invite a rotating shareholder to co-lead these reviews, ensuring all voices are integrated into operational planning. This should both sharpen discipline and build trust.

*2. Adapting if market shifts or real estate underperforms:*
If market conditions become less stable or real estate underperforms, I will introduce a formal scenario analysis at each quarterly meeting, weighing alternative assets more seriously. I will not hesitate to pivot our strategy if evidence shows a shift is warranted. My decision-making will remain practical and grounded in current realities, not habit.

---

**From Sofiane Qadir:**
*1. Balancing decisive leadership with greater consensus and input:*
I will introduce a pre-decision “alignment round” at each meeting, where all shareholders articulate their preferred option and rationale before I state my final position. This will surface valuable insights earlier and allow me to incorporate more perspectives before a decision is finalized.

*2. Steps to prioritize operational discipline and risk management:*
I will require each investment proposal to include a risk mitigation checklist and a simple operational execution plan, reviewed by at least one dissenting shareholder. This ensures discipline is not sidelined and risk management is embedded in every decision.

---

**From Darija Vukadinović:**
*1. Concrete steps for operational discipline and consistent execution:*
Quarterly post-mortems will be mandatory, with transparent tracking of execution against plan. Any quarter with flat or negative results will trigger a focused improvement cycle, with specific measures and responsibilities assigned and reported back at the next meeting.

*2. Balancing decisive leadership with greater engagement to avoid split votes:*
I will trial a two-step voting process: first, a non-binding straw poll to surface consensus, then a final vote after a facilitated round of discussion focused on closing gaps. This should reduce repeated deadlocks and improve buy-in.

---

**From Nerissa Okoye-Badran:**
*1. Balancing decisive leadership with engagement and consensus-building:*
I will make it standard practice to summarize not just the majority view but also the key arguments from the minority before making any final decision. This ensures all perspectives are respected and increases the likelihood of consensus.

*2. Steps to address operational discipline and prevent repeat lapses:*
For every flat or negative quarter, a root-cause analysis will be conducted and corrective actions tracked. Progress will be reported transparently to all shareholders, and persistent issues will trigger a re-evaluation of asset selection criteria.

---

**From Suhaila Mendonsa:**
*1. Ensuring shareholder input is meaningfully incorporated:*
I will formalize a process where every shareholder submits a brief written rationale alongside their vote, which will be summarized and considered prior to any override. Overrides will be reserved for situations where there is a clear, documented rationale for urgency or risk.

*2. Addressing operational discipline and a more cautious approach:*
I will pilot a “discipline-first” quarter next year, where we deliberately select the asset with the strongest operational reliability case, regardless of momentum, to demonstrate our ability to execute consistently and build credibility.

---

3. Leadership Self-Assessment

- **Leadership Effectiveness:** I have been effective in keeping meetings focused and moving decisively, but I recognize that my approach has sometimes limited consensus and engagement. I need to do more to bring the group along with me, not just set direction.
- **Decision Quality:** My decisions were grounded in available data and market conditions, but I acknowledge that a more adaptive approach—especially after underperformance—could have captured greater returns.
- **Communication Clarity:** I am confident in my ability to communicate options, data, and rationale clearly and directly.
- **Responsiveness to Input:** I have actively listened and summarized opposing views but have not always integrated them into final decisions. This is an area for improvement.
- **Adaptability:** I have favored consistency and momentum, but I must be quicker to adapt when evidence suggests a shift is needed.

---

4. Company Performance Assessment

- **Financial Results:** We achieved positive growth (7.69%) and avoided major losses, but captured only 54.8% of possible returns—there is clear room for improvement.
- **Risk Management:** We maintained a practical, disciplined approach, but operational discipline and risk mitigation were not systematically embedded, as shown by Q2’s flat result.
- **Strategic Alignment:** Our investments were consistent with our stated strategy and market conditions, but lacked sufficient flexibility to fully exploit market opportunities.
- **Capital Utilization:** We efficiently deployed capital each quarter, avoiding unnecessary conservatism.
- **Market Responsiveness:** Our approach was steady but not sufficiently responsive to changing performance signals or asset opportunities.

---

5. Future Direction: Improvements for Next Year

1. **Operational Discipline:**
- Implement quarterly post-mortems and root-cause analyses for any underperformance, with transparent action tracking and accountability.
- Require every investment proposal to include a risk mitigation checklist and operational execution plan, reviewed by dissenting shareholders.

2. **Shareholder Engagement & Consensus-Building:**
- Introduce alignment rounds and two-step voting to surface consensus and reduce repeated split votes.
- Require written rationales from all shareholders, with minority perspectives formally summarized before final decisions.

3. **Adaptive Strategy:**
- Conduct formal scenario analyses at each meeting and be prepared to pivot asset selection if market conditions or performance data indicate a need for change.
- Pilot at least one “discipline-first” quarter to demonstrate our ability to execute with reliability and build credibility.

---

In closing, I remain committed to decisive, practical leadership—but I recognize the need to more systematically embed operational discipline and shareholder engagement in our process. I expect everyone to bring the same level of directness and clarity to future discussions, and I will do my part to ensure your input is not just heard but acted upon. We have a solid foundation, but we can—and will—do better next year. Thank you for your trust and candor."

Performance Ratings


Annual Review Rating Results

CEO Performance Ratings

Leadership Effectiveness: 3.4/5

- Darael Mukendi: 4/5 - The CEO provided clear, decisive direction throughout the year, consistently guiding the group toward action even when consensus was lacking, but needs to improve on building broader shareholder buy-in.
- Sofiane Qadir: 3/5 - The CEO maintained clear direction and decisiveness, but repeatedly overriding split votes limited group effectiveness and eroded collaborative engagement.
- Darija Vukadinović: 4/5 - The CEO provided strong, decisive leadership with clear direction, consistently steering the group toward timely decisions even in the face of split votes.
- Nerissa Okoye-Badran: 3/5 - The CEO maintained clear direction and decisive leadership throughout the year, but repeatedly overriding split votes limited group effectiveness and undermined shareholder engagement.
- Suhaila Mendonsa: 3/5 - The CEO maintained basic direction and kept meetings focused, but repeatedly overrode shareholder votes and failed to build genuine consensus, limiting overall group effectiveness.

Decision Quality: 3.4/5

- Darael Mukendi: 4/5 - Final decisions were grounded in available information and market conditions, supporting company growth, though sometimes missed higher returns available from alternative assets.
- Sofiane Qadir: 3/5 - Decisions were grounded in available information and market conditions, but persisting with real estate after a flat quarter showed only average balance of risk and return.
- Darija Vukadinović: 4/5 - Decisions were grounded in practical reasoning and market conditions, though missed opportunities in certain quarters show room for more adaptive risk-taking.
- Nerissa Okoye-Badran: 3/5 - Decisions were generally grounded in available information and market conditions, but often prioritized momentum over operational discipline, leading to missed opportunities for more optimal risk-adjusted returns.
- Suhaila Mendonsa: 3/5 - Decisions were adequately grounded in available information and market conditions, but consistently prioritized momentum over proven discipline, resulting in only average outcomes.

Communication Clarity: 4.4/5

- Darael Mukendi: 5/5 - Communication was consistently clear, direct, and practical, with strong explanations of investment options, performance data, and reasoning at every meeting.
- Sofiane Qadir: 4/5 - Explanations of investment options, meeting summaries, and decision rationales were consistently clear and well-structured, with only minor issues.
- Darija Vukadinović: 5/5 - Explanations of options, performance data, and rationale were consistently clear, direct, and easy to follow throughout the year.
- Nerissa Okoye-Badran: 4/5 - Communication was consistently clear, with thorough explanations of investment options and rationales, though occasionally lacked sufficient emphasis on dissenting viewpoints.
- Suhaila Mendonsa: 4/5 - Communication was generally clear and direct when presenting options and rationales, with minor issues in fully addressing dissenting viewpoints.

Responsiveness to Input: 2.6/5

- Darael Mukendi: 3/5 - The CEO acknowledged shareholder perspectives and summarized opposing views, but often failed to fully incorporate dissenting input or adjust course when concerns persisted.
- Sofiane Qadir: 2/5 - While shareholder perspectives were acknowledged, input from dissenting voices was rarely incorporated into final decisions, especially regarding operational discipline and risk management.
- Darija Vukadinović: 3/5 - Input from shareholders was considered and acknowledged, but not always fully incorporated, particularly when votes were overridden without broader consensus.
- Nerissa Okoye-Badran: 3/5 - The CEO considered shareholder perspectives and summarized opposing views, but valuable input was rarely incorporated into final decisions, especially from the minority.
- Suhaila Mendonsa: 2/5 - Shareholder perspectives were minimally acknowledged and rarely incorporated into final decisions, as evidenced by repeated overrides of split votes and limited integration of dissenting input.

Adaptability: 2.6/5

- Darael Mukendi: 3/5 - The CEO maintained a consistent strategy but was slow to adapt or pivot after underperformance or changing signals, such as after the flat Q2 result.
- Sofiane Qadir: 2/5 - The CEO showed limited adaptation, persisting with the same strategy even after evidence of underperformance and failing to pivot or adjust in response to changing results.
- Darija Vukadinović: 3/5 - The CEO maintained a consistent strategy but was slow to adapt following underperformance, missing chances to pivot or diversify when evidence suggested it.
- Nerissa Okoye-Badran: 3/5 - The CEO demonstrated adequate adaptation to major issues but was slow to adjust strategy after underperformance and tended to favor consistency over flexibility.
- Suhaila Mendonsa: 2/5 - The CEO showed limited adaptation to changing circumstances, persisting with the same strategy even after underperformance and without sufficient adjustment to shareholder concerns.

Overall CEO Rating: 3.3/5

Company Performance Ratings

Financial Results: 2.8/5

- Darael Mukendi: 3/5 - The company achieved positive growth but captured only about half of the maximum possible returns given market conditions.
- Sofiane Qadir: 2/5 - The company somewhat underperformed market opportunities, capturing only 54.8% of possible returns despite a stable market environment.
- Darija Vukadinović: 3/5 - The company achieved moderate growth but only captured about half of the potential returns available in the market.
- Nerissa Okoye-Badran: 3/5 - The company achieved average returns given market conditions, capturing only about half of the maximum possible gains.
- Suhaila Mendonsa: 3/5 - The company achieved average returns (7.69% growth) but captured only about half of the potential market opportunity, missing out on higher, more consistent performance.

Risk Management: 3.0/5

- Darael Mukendi: 4/5 - The company maintained a practical balance of risk and return, avoiding major losses and unnecessary volatility, though operational discipline gaps were exposed in Q2.
- Sofiane Qadir: 3/5 - Risk and return were balanced adequately overall, but repeated real estate investments exposed the company to unnecessary risk after a flat quarter.
- Darija Vukadinović: 3/5 - Risk and return were balanced adequately overall, but operational discipline lapses in Q2 exposed the company to avoidable execution risk.
- Nerissa Okoye-Badran: 3/5 - Risk and return were balanced adequately overall, but repeated exposure to operational risk and lack of a safety buffer limited effectiveness.
- Suhaila Mendonsa: 2/5 - The company often failed to balance risk and return appropriately, repeatedly prioritizing momentum over operational discipline and exposing itself to unnecessary volatility.

Strategic Alignment: 3.6/5

- Darael Mukendi: 4/5 - Investment choices were strongly aligned with the stated strategy of practical, momentum-driven growth in a stable market, with only minor inconsistencies in adapting to missed opportunities.
- Sofiane Qadir: 3/5 - Investment choices moderately aligned with the stated strategy, but lacked flexibility and occasionally deviated from prudent operational discipline.
- Darija Vukadinović: 4/5 - Investment choices were strongly aligned with the stated strategy and market context, with only minor deviations from optimal flexibility.
- Nerissa Okoye-Badran: 4/5 - Investment decisions were mostly consistent with stated strategies and market conditions, with only minor inconsistencies in adapting to performance signals.
- Suhaila Mendonsa: 3/5 - Investment choices moderately aligned with the stated strategy, but there were occasional deviations from disciplined, risk-managed execution.

Capital Utilization: 4.4/5

- Darael Mukendi: 5/5 - Capital was fully and efficiently deployed each quarter, maximizing exposure to growth opportunities and avoiding unnecessary conservatism.
- Sofiane Qadir: 4/5 - Capital was efficiently deployed each quarter with no excessive conservatism, though not always for optimal gain.
- Darija Vukadinović: 5/5 - Capital was deployed efficiently and fully each quarter, maximizing exposure and avoiding unnecessary conservatism.
- Nerissa Okoye-Badran: 4/5 - Capital was deployed efficiently each quarter, avoiding unnecessary conservatism and ensuring steady asset growth.
- Suhaila Mendonsa: 4/5 - Capital was efficiently deployed each quarter without unnecessary conservatism, but efficiency was offset by missed opportunities for better risk-adjusted returns.

Market Responsiveness: 2.6/5

- Darael Mukendi: 3/5 - The company maintained a steady approach but was not sufficiently responsive to performance signals or alternative opportunities in quarters where other assets outperformed.
- Sofiane Qadir: 2/5 - The company was slow to adapt to performance signals, persisting with real estate despite clear indications that alternative assets would have improved results.
- Darija Vukadinović: 3/5 - The company maintained a steady approach but did not fully adjust to changing performance signals or asset opportunities when they arose.
- Nerissa Okoye-Badran: 3/5 - The company responded adequately to the stable market environment but failed to pivot quickly enough when performance signals indicated a need for change.
- Suhaila Mendonsa: 2/5 - The company was slow to adapt to market signals and underperformance, persisting with the same approach despite evidence that a shift was warranted.

Overall Company Rating: 3.3/5

Meeting Conclusion


CEO Tafadzwa Ndlovu concluded the meeting:

"Tafadzwa Ndlovu …appreciates the direct feedback reflected in these ratings. Our strengths are clear communication and decisive capital deployment, which have helped us build a solid operational foundation. At the same time, it’s obvious we need to improve how we incorporate shareholder input, adapt to changing signals, and embed more operational discipline. Next year, I will implement quarterly post-mortems, formalize alignment rounds before key decisions, and require risk mitigation plans for every investment. I expect us to move forward with more discipline and genuine engagement, building on our momentum to achieve stronger, more consistent results. Thank you for your candor—I’m confident we’ll deliver even better outcomes together in the year ahead."

The annual review meeting has concluded.
